data-go=\"173\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"x026c03","title":"The Prince with Too Many Pockets","volume":"婚后外传：A Tribute with Feathers","anchor":"chapter-0174","paragraphs":["At the evening performance, Mara discovered why the Supreme Court had been made of owls. Each cardboard judge swiveled its head when the defendant lied. The script began as a thriller, but the first owl fell over at the mention of a stolen visa. The audience immediately understood the company's preferred tense: anything might happen next. At eight o'clock the audience was already cheering. Its singular hero wasted no time bargaining for freedom.","Cassian sat beside her, looking much too pleased. \"A tribute,\" he repeated.","\"A threatening word.\"","The foreign-born chorus leader had made a game of the alphabet. Each child chose an adverb to perform, and the smallest boy selected 'magnificently.' The result was not obscure. His magnificence could be heard in the foyer. The director's wish for acclaim had become a very loud reality.","\"You'll survive the first verse.\" The catalog typically gave him only two lines to explain himself.","Lena began her Spanish song, its southwestern rhythm slower than the frantic rehearsal loop. The translation appeared on a screen beneath a clear subheading. A vowel that had troubled the young chorus now sounded effortless. Their teacher had reduced the topic to a few useful movements, then trusted them to sing. Skilled instruction did not have to predetermine every breath.","The story's missing treaty turned out to be a love letter written in the wrong verb form. A telegram supplied the correction. A new statute ordered every person in love to stop talking like a lawyer. Mara glanced at Cassian, who had the grace to look wounded. The laughter that followed was her favorite part of the play, until the heroine appeared.","She wore a black coat, carried a stolen silver spoon, and had an alarming amount of wit. Her first utterance accused the prince of having too many pockets. The resemblance was impossible to miss. Mara covered her face. Cassian gently lowered her hands so he could see her expression. Lena became vocal about how well the chorus had sung.","\"You subsidised this?\"","\"A small contribution.\"","Mara's speculation about Lena had been materialistic in its own odd way: she had looked at a box before looking at her husband. She would not be deprived of the opportunity to laugh at herself. The retired actor, now point person for anything with feathers, called it a spur to future comedy. Cassian looked as if the suggestion pleased him dangerously.","\"Define small.\"","\"The financial topic seems less romantic.\"","The heroine solved the mystery by climbing through the wrong window and refusing to surrender the spoon. A signpost was promptly carried onstage reading WRONG WINDOW. The actor's yell brought a second owl down. Mara laughed until the jacket slipped from her shoulders. Cassian restored it without looking away from her.","At the interval, Theo found his fiancée near the drinks. He had returned the empty star box to Lena and abandoned his roof proposal. The off-plan apartment brochure lay folded in his pocket. He told his partner what he had done, without a performance, and asked what she wanted. She looked furious for a minute, then began asking sensible questions. It was not the moment he had imagined, but it was theirs to weigh up together.","Lena wore the recovered star against a dark scarf. She would pass down its story as well as the jewel, she announced. Mara suspected the jackdaw would become larger with every telling. The actor drawing the cartoon had already endowed it with royal wings. No speculation in the foyer could improve the bird's actual achievement: one bright thing collected from an open window.","Cassian's tickets to the Royal Shakespeare Company attracted an envious sigh from a visiting student. He offered her the afternoon program they could no longer use, after checking that the seats could be transferred. Mara watched the girl's face light up. His grand gestures to her delighted her; his small attention to strangers made them feel less like a performance.","The second half began with signaling from the orchestra pit. A drummer tapped a temporal cue, the curtain rose, and the retired actor resumed his role as an owl's interpreter. His shorthand notes were visible from the front row. Mara could make out only one phrase: DO NOT YELL BEFORE THE BIRD FALLS. It seemed sound professional advice.","In the final scene, the heroine asked the prince to stop trying to surprise her. He promised to try, and immediately produced a cake from beneath his cloak. The comedy had not attempted a realistic anatomy of royalty. It offered a man who was hopelessly in love and a woman clever enough to enjoy it. Mara had no objection to that genre at all.","An under-performing spotlight flickered during the last bow. The stagehand shifted to a second lamp, and the lovers remained visible. Theo took his fiancée's hand in the wings; the apartment brochure had disappeared, but she was still there. The audience applauded as if it could see the private reconciliation too.","Outside, Cassian opened the taxi door. The driver adjusted the rearview mirror, recognized Mara from the morning, and wisely asked no questions. She held the little play's program against her chest.","\"Did I ruin your surprise?\" she asked.","\"You improved it. There was no bird in my original plan.\"","\"Careless planning.\"","He pulled her close, his mouth warm against her temple. \"I'll weigh up the criticism.\"","The city lights moved across the glass. Mara let the program slide onto the seat and kissed her husband until the driver became intensely interested in the next traffic light. Some tributes, she decided, could be answered without words."],"questions":[],"wordCount":934,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0174\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：A had stolen jewels, escaped armed men, and married a duke. None of these accomplishments prepared her to roll pastry while Cassian watched. \"You look determined,\" he said. Their donations were already well above the target. Bram later offered an insincere compliment whose meaning was plainly an insult.","\"I am determined to discover why this dough has a grudge.\"","His giggle was deeply unhelpful. She dusted his sleeve with flour, and the nearby documentary crew decided this was the footage they wanted.","The River Table contest had occupied the old market since the turn of the century. Its recipe handbook included a cake created for workers building the bridge. A historical legend claimed the first winner had baked it inside a locomotive. The museum's documentary excerpt treated that story as a myth, although the annual slogan still used a train. Nobody wanted to rewrite a beloved poster merely because the evidence was imperfect.","June had entered undercover, disguised as a cheerful amateur who was in fact a terrible amateur. Her day of data analysis involved a questionnaire about portions and the systematic destruction of three eggs. She hoped the contest would produce an essay about local traditions. The editorial deadline made her passionate about finishing; the smell made her passionate about lunch.","June's quarterly food column was a new venture in journalism. Its English editor wanted warmth, not a sermon. The assignment did not purport to explain every regional custom. June could say hello to the cooks and goodbye to her anonymity afterwards. 'Okay,' she said, 'but no one may obsess over my crust.' The pact sounded safe until Cassian inspected it.","Their neighbor, Eve Kellan, was pregnant and competing for the last time before a planned break. Her partner, Hal, offered to carry the flour. She accepted, then forbade him to recite the safety handbook at her. She had read the relevant guideline, spoken to her clinician about the day, and arranged a chair. Mara liked her immediately. Help did not require everyone to repeat a warning.","Eve's rival, Bram, had a serial cooking show and a habit of treating every bowl as a camera opportunity. His lineup of assistants included a man with a law degree who had abandoned an exam in commercial practice to become a food writer. Bram introduced him as \"our legal safety net.\" The man rolled his eyes and corrected the oven temperature.","A forecast of rain sent volunteers moving tables beneath the roof. Mara helped a boy pedal a cargo bicycle into cover, then watched the program's sponsor overbid for a commemorative apron. The auction was ridiculous but useful: the money supported a soup kitchen. Eve's own savings were illiquid, tied up in a small flat, and she could not afford a new mixer. Winning the equipment prize would matter to her.","The judges' mandate emphasized taste, not drama. The shortlist would be chosen by anonymous samples. Its format concealed the cooks' names until the final round, though the colorful booths made anonymity a partial fiction. A woman judging the cakes had written an English essay on food history; another taught Greek. Their interests could not tell Mara whose tart they would prefer. Hal had to readjust the stand before the next tray arrived.","Bram insisted that Eve's recipe was obsolete. \"You may well finish last with that.\"","She shrugged. \"I may. It's still mine.\"","The retired baker's judgement was that Bram's negative remarks followed a double standard. He praised a man's stubbornness while mocking Eve's. Such nonsense deserved reproach, though a shouting match would be pointless. The forecast made rain foreseeable; it did not make gender prejudice inevitable. Mara hid a postcard advertising the memorial dinner beneath her bowl before flour could cover it.","The answer earned encouragement from a retired baker. Mara wanted to applaud, but her own pastry had begun to glide off the board. Cassian caught it with the speed of a man intercepting a falling knife. The crew's laughter ended in a collective sigh when he rescued the crust intact. \"Exceptional reflexes,\" Mara told him. \"A pity the filling is yours.\"","\"Rude.\"","\"Accurate.\"","Their cheesy quarrel relieved Eve's tension. She was skillful enough to rebalance a tray with one hand, but not immune to a rival's pressure. Bram had loudly predicted that pregnancy would impair her judgment. The gender stereotype was an unacceptable substitution for assessing her work. Hal's face darkened. Eve stopped him with a look and went back to measuring.","The recipe card required a subtraction from a larger batch. Mara nearly forgot to subtract the reserved flour. Eve caught the mistake without making a performance of it. She would not rush through her own work merely to keep pace with the television chef. Bram's team, meanwhile, had used a wasteful quantity of disposable bowls, shifting the cleanup onto the volunteers. June marked the detail for her story.","A bell announced the first judging. An organizer handed out a reminder of the prohibition on altering submitted samples. The provision seemed straightforward. Then Eve discovered that her tin carried a different entry number. The manifest listed her as using nuts she had never included. A juror warned that this could end in disqualification.","Eve asked for the original registration. The organizer offered a cursory paraphrase of the rule instead. She had not moaned once about the heat, but now her voice tightened. The precondition for a fair contest was an accurate entry. A mistaken label should not become an indictment of the cook.","Mara moved beside her. \"Let's find the right card.\"","Bram's assistant gave a little reproachful sigh. \"Don't make a scene.\"","\"I'm asking for paper,\" Eve answered. \"You can survive the confrontation.\"","Cassian tried to convince the organizer to pause the judging. The request was reasonable, and the woman agreed. Mara noticed the price tag from Bram's kit caught beneath Eve's tin. Her suspicion was suggestive, not conclusive; perhaps the kits had simply shared a packing table. She would not overestimate its importance.","June offered to compare the original forms. Her supposed amateur innocence had become less convincing by the minute. \"A milestone,\" Cassian whispered. \"She has discovered a use for being undercover that doesn't involve eating her own cake.\"","Mara smiled and reached for the registration box."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1043,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0175\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">伙伴外传：The Table","anchor":"chapter-0176","paragraphs":["The registration box held a postcard, a shopping receipt, and Eve's actual form. Mara found them beneath a glossy leaflet for Bram's cooking drama. The form showed her ingredients clearly. Its serial number differed from the tin's label by one digit. No elaborate data analysis was needed to see the mismatch, but the organizer asked them to wait for the head judge before proceeding. The leaflet did not purport to settle the dispute. The English wording of the regulation was clear. Its meaning needed no sermon. A forecast promised sunshine; the judge gave a sigh. Bram's gender jokes earned a look of reproach from every woman nearby.","Eve sat beside Hal, one hand against her pregnant belly. He offered to fetch water, and she accepted with a grateful smile. She remained passionate about the contest; she did not have to stand up to prove it. June set her own ruinous pastry aside and helped a volunteer rebalance the entry trays.","The head judge had written the recipe handbook. Her recollection of the event stretched back to a memorial dinner for the bridge builders. \"At the turn of the century, they judged puddings by candlelight,\" she said. \"We should manage a printed number.\"","'Hello again,' the judge said, reading the price tag on Bram's sponsor apron. 'Did you overbid for the privilege of advertising?' The question brought a giggle from Eve. Mara's judgement improved: she liked this woman. The judge's guideline mentioned a prohibition on tampering, and its provision was clear. Bram's violation was an indictment of his behavior, not his whole profession.","She took a photograph of the disputed label, checked the manifest, and corrected the entry. There was no disqualification. Bram gave a dramatic moan about the delay, then suggested that Eve had been overestimating her ability to compete. His assistant with the law degree looked embarrassed. A cooking exam did not improve because the loudest candidate supplied the verdict.","Mara asked who had delivered the tins. The assistant had collected them together. He admitted placing the wrong label, then claimed it was a harmless mistake. Eve accepted the first part and questioned the second. A false ingredient warning could damage her work and mislead a person choosing what to eat. It was not harmless simply because someone had caught it.","Bram ordered him to stop talking. The man shrugged, the shrug more eloquent than the instruction. He finally said Bram had asked him to switch the numbers so Eve's sample would miss the first judging. The false allergy detail was an unintended result of moving a preprinted label. He would give the organizer a written account. Mara left that conversation to them; the next bell had rung.","\"No rush through the finish,\" Eve told herself.","The television chef's last-minute showmanship had created a foreseeable problem. His enormous display cake blocked a safe route between tables. A volunteer asked him to shift it onto the side stand. He refused, then caught his sleeve on its sugar arch. The structure began to tilt. Mara caught the lower tray while Cassian took the top. Eve, closest to the wobbling stand, cleared the tools away and steadied its feet. Her skillful movement saved a cake belonging to a man who had just tried to remove her.","Bram stared at his intact masterpiece. His first words were about the camera angle.","The retired baker might well have delivered a sermon about manners. Instead, he said, 'Okay, let's keep the children away from the hot trays.' The double standard had upset him; the immediate work mattered too. His assistant began a quiet song, and the familiar tune let a boy stop crying. Eve's technique remained well above Bram's predictions.","\"You're welcome,\" Eve said.","June had intended a gentle essay. Her new editorial would still describe the food, but no safety net of politeness could conceal that moment. She asked the documentary crew for their footage rather than relying on gossip. Their format would include the entire exchange, not an excerpt arranged to make a rescue look like sabotage. June would not shift the blame onto the young volunteer.","A young volunteer brought a questionnaire to Mara. It asked whether she had enjoyed the event's inclusive atmosphere. Mara laughed too sharply, then apologized to the girl. The question was not her invention. She chose an honest answer and returned the paper. Public encouragement was not a mandate to call every decision good.","Bram's assistant quit his role on the cooking show. His savings were as illiquid as Eve's, he admitted, so the gesture frightened him. But staying would mean defending another stunt tomorrow. He asked the retired baker about weekend work. The man offered an introduction, without promising a place that did not yet exist.","The final required a small pastry from the historical recipe. Its legend of a locomotive oven survived in the program, despite the museum's more cautious account. June planned a funny paragraph about that myth. The tradition could bear a joke. She would paraphrase the old quotation accurately rather than convert it into a fact by repetition.","Cassian read the Greek name on one contestant's sweet. His pronunciation was apparently appalling. Mara corrected him with an authority she did not possess, and the contestant laughed at both of them. The joke ended in a shared tasting spoon. Mara's pleasure in the contest improved markedly once somebody else was doing the baking.","Eve had to subtract a small allowance from the cooking time because her oven ran hot. She checked her test piece before making the adjustment. The arithmetic would not be allowed to rush her hands. Hal watched without adding instructions. His quiet faith had become her best encouragement.","June's undercover role was officially finished when the presenter recognized her from a journalism panel. The lineup of celebrity guests abruptly acquired a reporter with flour on her forehead. \"An exceptional investigative technique,\" Cassian said.","\"I may need a new day job,\" she answered.","The crowd's humor helped settle the delay. A child delivered a handmade farewell card to Eve, having misunderstood her planned break as retirement. She thanked him and explained that she would return. The conversation made her blink hard. Hal took the card before flour could smudge it.","Bram's slogan promised perfection. Eve's final tray promised only the food she knew how to make. Mara preferred its modest honesty. The shortlist was announced, and Eve's name came first. Her gasp was so delighted that Cassian smiled before the applause even began."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1082,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0176\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">伙伴外传：The River Table</div><h1>The Cake That Nearly Fell</h1><p id=\"chapter-0176-p1\"><a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">The</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-6886\" data-words=\"6886\">registration</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1072\" data-words=\"1072\">box</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3954\" data-words=\"3954\">held</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-6320\" data-words=\"6320\">postcard</a>, <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-7506\" data-words=\"7506,7503\">shopping</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-6794\" data-words=\"6794\">receipt</a>, <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-383\" data-words=\"383\">and</a> Eve&#x27;s <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-138\" data-words=\"138\">actual</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3378\" data-words=\"3378\">form</a>. Mara had expected the judges to reward a taller structure, but they chose the pastry that made them reach for a second spoon. The final verdict was unanimous. Eve sat down before her knees could turn the moment into slapstick, and Hal knelt beside her with the prize card. Her winning score was well above her own hopeful estimate. Hal's humor returned. Their pact had survived the day, and he would not shift the burden onto her. A volunteer began to pedal the cargo bicycle home. Cassian told her not to overestimate his wife's appetite for another contest. Mara called that nonsense and stole a cherry.","\"We won a mixer,\" she said.","\"You won a mixer. I carried flour.\"","She pulled him up and kissed him hard enough to make a child cover his eyes. Mara approved. Cassian looked at her with a question she answered by taking his hand.","The head judge asked Eve to speak briefly. The pregnant cook rose when she felt ready, with Hal beside her. She said she had been passionate about baking long before television made it glamorous. Her final cake came from a family notebook written near the turn of the century. She had changed the measurements, the oven, and one ingredient her grandmother could not have bought. Tradition had survived because people used it, not because they never touched it.","The quarterly column would have to be rewritten. June had brought a questionnaire, not expected a lesson in gossip or a milestone in her friendships. 'A negative review of my own baking is unavoidable,' she admitted. 'But I will not obsess over it.' Eve's laughter helped her readjust. She could glide past the failure without pretending to be good.","The judge wanted the recipe for next year's handbook. Eve agreed after checking that her name would appear with it. The prize would help her rebalance paid work with the time she wanted at home. It did not require her to promise a heroic schedule after the birth. She could be ambitious without making exhaustion part of the contract.","Bram had been removed from the final after the assistant's account and the video were reviewed. He left before the awards. His departure was quieter than his arrival, although the documentary crew recorded it. June would use only what the story needed. The cook did not become less talented because he had behaved badly; the talent did not excuse the sabotage.","A volunteer asked Mara whether she regretted saving his cake. \"Not at all,\" she said. \"It would have landed on somebody.\" The organizer was determined to rewrite that routine.","Cassian added, \"And it looked expensive. My wife has an instinct for rescuing valuables.\"","She elbowed him. \"I rescued you once.\"","The organizer gave Bram's insincere apology to the assistant with the law degree. He called its suggestive blame-shifting unacceptable. His own goodbye was polite, and he meant it. A reminder of the regulation would go into next year's program. The former assistant would be able to apply for an ordinary stall if he wished, with no need to defend a celebrity.","\"An excellent investment.\"","The organizer offered to send Eve the corrected registration before publishing the winners. She had rushed through the first explanation and nearly made the error sound like Eve's fault. Now she apologized plainly. The cook accepted without pretending the afternoon had been pleasant from start to finish. Her skillful recovery belonged to her, and she was allowed to be proud of it. Her undercover disguise had been a pointless pretense for hours.","The boy with the farewell card returned carrying a paper mixer he had made from a box. He offered to demonstrate it. Its cardboard handle broke on the second turn. Eve admired it anyway, and Hal promised a repair with tape. The boy looked relieved. A perfect machine had not been a precondition of being thanked.","June finally tasted her own entry. The result made her close her eyes. \"I should subtract several years from my baking career.\"","Mara peeled a price tag from the prize brochure and handed Eve a clean postcard for her family. It bore a quotation from the old market song. Afterwards, Hal hummed the tune while they packed. The fuss was wasteful, June said, but the cake was worth it. The praise lacked his usual cheesy polish, and Eve knew it was sincere.","\"Several decades,\" Cassian said. The forecaster may well have underestimated the evening breeze.","Mara tasted a crumb, then handed the plate back with great care. \"Its texture could support a small bridge.\"","Eve laughed until she had to wipe her eyes. Hal offered to keep the cake as a doorstop. June refused to let her first attempt become a permanent public monument. She wrapped it for disposal, then helped clean the table. The waste bothered her more than the mockery. Next time she would practice a small batch before feeding strangers.","As the stalls closed, Mara watched the families divide their leftovers. An old man gave away a tin he had carried to the market for forty years. Two teenagers traded bread for fruit. The contest's loudest dispute was already shrinking beside the ordinary business of people making sure someone else ate.","Cassian found Mara's rejected crust in their basket. He broke off a corner, considered it solemnly, and said it had potential.","\"You're lying because you love me.\"","\"I contain a wide range of talents.\"","She took the piece from him and tried it. It was dreadful. He kissed her before she could say so, tasting of sugar and coffee. Their laughter drew an indulgent look from Eve, who had stopped to rest by the door.","\"You two should enter again,\" the winner said.","\"A cruel proposal,\" Mara answered.","\"I'll coach you.\"","Cassian offered to carry the equipment whenever that future lesson took place. Mara would not rush through accepting a challenge from the woman who had beaten the professional. She looked at her husband, at the broken crust, and at the winner's tired, shining face.","\"All right,\" she said. \"But next time, he rolls the dough.\"","Cassian bowed as if she had given him a kingdom. The market bell rang above them, and they walked out into the last warm light."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1053,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0177\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">伙伴外传：The River Table</div><h1>A Mixer and a Kingdom</h1><p id=\"chapter-0177-p1\">Eve&#x27;s <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-9243\" data-words=\"9243\">winning</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1210\" data-words=\"1210\">cake</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-4940\" data-words=\"4940\">looked</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-314\" data-words=\"314\">almost</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-6194\" data-words=\"6194\">plain</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-923\" data-words=\"923\">beneath</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-4847\" data-words=\"4847\">lights</a>. a concert, and one night in the inn's best suite. Cassian could have bought the entire inn's attention. Mara wanted the medal. \"It has a donkey on it,\" she said. \"A powerful incentive.\"","Her husband examined the illustrated beast. \"A striking likeness to several people I know.\"","They stood in a rural, forested valley beneath a northern hill. The old bookshop had devised the contest to fund its roof. Its owner, Annel, refused to underfund the children's library to pay for repairs elsewhere. Well-heeled visitors could donate outright; everyone else could compete. The public invitation was aimed at readers of every age, and Mara had come in boots rather than silk.","Against the background of the dark ridge, the bookshop seemed a spiritual refuge. Its most recent bestseller concerned a garden, and an actual flower stem marked Mara's copy. She wanted to be done with the opening instructions and begin. Simon, already obsessed with the scoring criterion, demanded an impartial timekeeper. The assistant gave a reasoned answer: everyone would get the same limit.","A television crew recorded a cheerful opening. The TV show loved a fairy-tale couple, but Mara insisted on carrying her own pack. Its main compartment held a fable about a lost manuscript, a prehistoric animal model, and a damaged telecom handset. The phone was only a clue container; a card clearly said it did not work. Its missing warranty amused Cassian more than the rest of the kit.","The first clue asked for a past-tense verb from the bookshop's oldest textbook. Contestants had to recite the relevant line, then underline the word. Mara was skillful at reading a crowded page quickly; Cassian was skillful at making her forget what she had read. He leaned close to offer up an entirely irrelevant compliment. She pushed him away with a grin and found the verb successfully.","The instruction told them to find an article about careers before the US Supreme Court. A bibliography on the back listed several magazine issues. The law article appeared beside a western film review, a yearly symphony program, and a piece about the cherry-tree-chopping fable. Its illustration contained a small red flag, which matched a marker on the route map. No legal knowledge was needed. They had to look carefully rather than groan at the title.","A well-dressed competitor, Simon, called the premise childish. His young daughter, Bea, wanted to read the fable. He dictated their route anyway, quoting a definition of efficiency that gave imagination no place. Mara caught the girl's eye and winked. Bea hid a smile beneath her pamphlet.","The route crossed a meadow populated by painted wooden animals. A donkey guarded a mailbox. In accordance with the clue, each team inserted a ballot bearing its chosen answer. Correct entries earned a stamp, not a penalty for slower readers. Annel's curriculum of games was gentle enough to make a mistake useful. The spelling of one animal's name supplied the next direction.","Cassian selected the wrong beast deliberately, received a solemn correction from a child, and bowed. \"A valuable oral lesson.\"","\"Your honor survives,\" Mara said.","They reached the old telecom shed before Simon. Its deed hung on the wall beside a conservation license. Annel's family had converted it into a tiny reading room, with a monthly movie night and stationery for anyone who wanted to write home. The Inspectional Services Department had approved its new stairs last spring. A copy of the inspection hung where visitors could see it. The certificate was not a clue, though Simon tried to quote it into an answer.","A bold clause in the game protocol allowed teams to ask for help. Mara was convinced that Simon had not read it. He looked suited to winning things, yet the unanswered puzzle seemed daunting enough to make him quarrel with his own map. The sight made her want to steer Bea toward an easier companion.","The real clue concerned a shifting baseline. A sequence of landscape illustrations showed the woods growing thinner; each generation's postcard called its own scene \"unchanged.\" The contest asked them to identify the earliest picture, not decide which narrator sounded most confident. Mara found the old photograph behind a removable panel. It illuminated the source of the stream marked on their map.","A web-based answer box refused to load. Annel's assistant supplied the paper version immediately. The infrastructure failure did not warrant disqualifying anyone. A warranty on a router would not create a signal in a valley. Cassian borrowed a pencil from Mara and drew a compliment on her wrist: a tiny crown with a crooked star.","\"Meaningful scholarship,\" she observed.","\"A concise equation. You plus me equals trouble.\"","A rumour traveled up the trail that the final prize had been stolen. Mara nearly rushed toward the inn, then stopped. She had rushed to solve enough problems for a lifetime, and now she almost rushed to question the innkeeper. A proper question might uncover a simpler explanation. The assistant confirmed that someone had moved the medal to keep it out of rain. The scandal expired before it became a confrontation.","At the stream, a sign asked them to recite a little refrain before collecting the next page. Simon refused to sing. Bea began alone, then faltered. Mara joined her, and Cassian supplied an absurdly deep final note. Simon looked irritated, then a little ashamed. The child earned the stamp for her team.","Mara's paper map now pointed into the trees. Its formula for the route resembled a prescription written by a distracted doctor. Cassian offered up his arm; she preferred his hand. They walked into the forest, exceptionally pleased with the afternoon.","Behind them, Simon reread the first clue. His daughter waited without reproach. The next part of the hunt had finally become something he might share."],"questions":[],"wordCount":972,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0178\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：The Donkey Medal</div><h1>The Donkey Is the Prize</h1><p id=\"chapter-0178-p1\"><a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">The</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-9333\" data-words=\"9333\">yearly</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8656\" data-words=\"8656\">treasure</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-4044\" data-words=\"4044\">hunt</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5677\" data-words=\"5677\">offered</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-9275\" data-words=\"9275\">wooden</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5135\" data-words=\"5135\">medal</a>, <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1720\" data-words=\"1720\">concert</a>, <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-383\" data-words=\"383\">and</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5722\" data-words=\"5722\">one</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5511\" data-words=\"5511\">night</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-4167\" data-words=\"4167\">in</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> inn&#x27;s <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-931\" data-words=\"931\">best</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8121\" data-words=\"8121\">suite</a>.
